# Hypervisor OT Compatibility — Published Compatibility Research (v2.4)

**Prepared by:** Priyanka Deva  
Merck Enterprise Technology Innovation  
Global Technology  
**As of:** 30 October 2026  
**Classification:** Internal use only  
**Research basis:** Public manufacturer documentation assessed against the 97 supplied supplier/application records; no independently verified installed estate.

## Executive research conclusion

**Published alternative-hypervisor support exists for selected OT software components, but is not transferable across an OEM's complete product portfolio.** Manufacturer evidence distinguishes explicitly named supported configurations, conditional support, generic VM deployment permission, component-specific exclusions and product/edition identities that remain unestablished in the inspected public evidence.

**Cloud leadership takeaways — documented evidence, not migration advice**

| Key finding | Demonstrated source-bound example | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| Named alternative support exists | Emerson DeltaV Virtual Studio documents defined Hyper-V deployment [S01]; Siemens TIA Portal V21 names ESXi and Hyper-V for relevant engineering components [S05]. | Named manufacturer support is component-, release- and configuration-specific. |
| One application family may have different results by component | Waters Empower general policy [S09] and FR5 restrictions [S10]; Agilent OpenLab CDS 3.0 component table [S12]. | Do not grant a supplier-wide approval from one supported component. |
| Virtualization is not universally mandatory | AMDT octoplant physical-server documentation [N27]; BioTek 405 TS onboard functionality [A46]. | Native/physical execution and optional virtualization are different claims. |
| Published exclusions must be retained | Cognex VisionPro Deep Learning / Deep Learning Service 4.2 excludes VM execution [N05]. | The exclusion must not be generalized to other Cognex products. |
| Many records are not independent hostable software applications | Custom integration/equipment examples include Industrial Automation Systems [N21], RoviSys [N22] and Alfa Laval [N23]. | Supplier/equipment records are outside direct hypervisor certification unless a distinct software component is named. |
| Evidence gaps do not mean product incompatibility | Numerous target-platform pairs are U, I or W in the retained research matrix. | No verified publication is not equivalent to an OEM statement of non-support. |

**Scope:** 97 supplied records, including grouped supplier/equipment/service items; 65 original grouped entries reconciled; 12 platform columns assessed in the retained technical evidence. These are *not* 97 proven VM workloads. Published backup interoperability is a separate domain, not proof of application hosting. No inference is made about installed versions, estate-wide compatibility, licensing or migration suitability.

## Three-question compatibility view (all 97 supplied records)

Question 1: **Is a hypervisor mandatory?** Say No only when a documented non-hypervisor route is established for the named scope; an equipment/supplier record may be not applicable. Question 2: **Is virtual-machine support explicitly published?** Distinguish named-platform, conditional, generic, explicit restriction and no position established. Question 3: **Which hypervisors are explicitly named?** Only list a named platform for the exact component, not from guest-OS support, marketing references, shared KVM technology or unrelated products.

**Critical interpretation:** The summary below is a report-layer index derived conservatively from the retained 12-platform register, not a new certification. A row can contain multiple components with contradictory outcomes; its detailed finding and original publication govern. The named-hypervisor field reflects the current source-register codes, *not* a full enterprise application support promise.

**Caution on mixed findings:** Each source entry can combine central servers, acquisition nodes, embedded instruments, back-up products and engineering software. The full component-specific exclusions and release conditions appear in the retained technical matrix and original source register; the summary does not supersede them. The source-backed execution statement remains separately available below.

## Research completion and preservation controls

This v2.4 synthesis changes the presentation hierarchy, not the underlying manufacturer support classifications. All original rows, detailed source URLs, support limitations, date qualifications and the complete 97-entry execution review remain retained below. There is no enterprise current-state claim, recommendation, migration checklist, readiness score, financial estimate or extrapolated percentage. The accompanying HTML v2.2 is a presentation derived from this canonical Markdown.

## Retained full research register — evidence and execution details

# Archived research and technical detail (preserved verbatim from preceding master)

**Prepared by:** Priyanka Deva  
Merck Enterprise Technology Innovation  
Global Technology  
**As of:** 30 October 2026  
**Classification:** Internal use only  
**Basis:** The Markdown is the sole research register. This revision adds a documented execution-requirement statement for each of the 97 records and separates that question from optional VM compatibility and backup interoperability. Original inventory identities and descriptions remain intact.

## Execution requirements — one statement per roster entry

**Leadership read:** requiring a hypervisor to run and being supported on a selected hypervisor are different questions. Octoplant has a documented physical-server option; it must not be presented as dependent on a hypervisor merely because a named-platform support statement is unresolved. [N27] The inspected versiondog requirements are historical and separately dated. [N28]

The default report view presents one plain-language execution statement per record, with sources and component/release boundaries. It does not assign requirement codes or repeat a requirement across twelve platform columns. Published VM compatibility and backup interoperability remain available in the separate **Published VM support** view and technical evidence matrix for cases where virtual deployment or VM protection is being considered.

Every supplied record was reviewed. The live route pass revisited 87 existing documentation routes; 83 returned readable material and four did not. Relevant installation, workstation, native/container and physical/VM passages were inspected in targeted follow-ups. Readable route counts are not a claim of complete inspection of every page of every manual. Product overviews do not establish technical prerequisites; unidentified applications and unconfirmed deployment editions remain explicitly unresolved. A Windows OS list alone is not proof of physical-only operation, and absence of a hypervisor in a prerequisite list is reported as that limited finding.

**Material distinction:** Project Configuration Tool 2 for Windows requires Hyper-V, while its Linux edition runs natively; this does not establish a Hyper-V requirement for all EcoStruxure production components. [S13] Appian 25.4+ self-managed cores require Kubernetes containers, which must not be equated with a mandatory hypervisor. [A22]

The original inventory identities and descriptions are preserved. A statement about a documented component or legacy release must not be inherited by an entire supplier, grouped equipment record or unidentified internal solution. The current installed estate is not certified by this execution review.

## Deployment applicability — leadership findings

**Supporting scope context:** the execution statements above take precedence for prerequisite wording; build any optional virtual-deployment qualification shortlist from identifiable software components. Keep physical equipment, supplier records, hosted cores and backup protection in their own decision scopes. An inventory of 97 supplier/application entries is not 97 VM migration candidates.

**Applicability comes before compatibility.** Identify the installed product/release and component role; inspect its deployment architecture, hardware/interface requirements and licensing; check explicit VM permissions or exclusions; then qualify the specific hypervisor/OS/database and OEM configuration. A USB/serial interface, Windows support, a network connection or a vendor name alone cannot establish VM eligibility or ineligibility.

The fresh pass reopened 78 existing documentation routes and inspected targeted manufacturer documentation, including controller requirements and relevant manual sections. The original three access-gap cases were investigated again. Readable Octoplant server/agent architecture, a Climet-authored client/server brochure and Roche-authored control-unit guidance now inform their deployment assessment. The two latter documents were inspected as clearly labelled manufacturer-authored republications, not treated as publisher-hosted certifications. Their original routes still have access/retrieval limitations recorded in the technical audit. No source-access problem is a compatibility classification.

### Applicability categories and decision rules

| Deployment category | Decision rule |
|---|---|
| Server software | Assess the named server/central software component; server architecture alone is not hypervisor approval. |
| Container application | Qualify the container/Kubernetes stack first; underlying node virtualization is a separate platform decision. |
| Instrument / control workstation | Check acquisition/control role, OEM hardware terms, drivers and device interfaces. Do not assume VM eligibility or physical-only operation. |
| Equipment record — isolate software | The physical equipment is not a VM-hosting subject. Identify any separately hosted HMI, data service or control software before assessing it. |
| Supplier / service record | Not applicable to the supplier/service record itself. Qualify the actual software delivered or hosted. |
| Provider-hosted core | Customer-selected hypervisor is not applicable to the published hosted core. Assess customer-operated connectors separately. |
| Backup protection | Compare protection methods and restore paths; backup interoperability is not application-hosting approval. |
| Mixed / deployment to confirm | Separate the components and confirm the software identity and deployment model before assigning applicability. |

These supporting deployment categories are inventory metadata, not hypervisor requirement classifications. The category describes the **assessment unit or lead listed below**, not every product sold by a supplier. Equipment-only and supplier records are outside direct VM-hosting assessment at that record level; attached or delivered software still needs assessment. Container applications retain infrastructure dependencies and are not automatically outside virtualization scope. A workstation remains a conditional candidate unless the manufacturer explicitly excludes the named component from VMs. Public product architecture does not independently establish how the internal installation is deployed.

### Material documentation findings

| Entry / component | Inspected evidence | Leadership treatment |
|---|---|---|
| Cognex VisionPro Deep Learning / Deep Learning Service 4.2 | Manufacturer supported-OS page explicitly excludes these components from VMs. [N05] | Keep the component-specific exclusion. Do not apply it to all Cognex systems or assume the unidentified installed product is that component. |
| BioTek 405 TS | Manufacturer sheet distinguishes onboard protocol functions from optional LHC2/LHC2 Secure computer control. [A46] | The instrument/onboard runtime is not a VM migration subject. Assess optional PC software separately; its VM eligibility remains unestablished. |
| SCIEX PA800 | January 2026 installation guide, p. 5 validates supplied controllers and limits support for customer PCs. [A31] | OEM-qualified control workstation. This is not a direct prohibition of every VM deployment. Biomek requires a separate component assessment. |
| Roche Cedex HiRes | Roche-authored October 2020 Addendum 6, software 2.3.2, recommends Roche control unit/image and limits support on other hardware. [N20] Current manufacturer product information was also inspected. [N19] | Treat as an OEM-constrained controller pending installed-release confirmation. Do not infer a universal VM ban, and do not label an old retrieval limit as an application finding. |
| Climet | Manufacturer-authored DataPro3 brochure identifies separate client, DAS and SQL roles; the brochure is republished by MedicalExpo. [N18] | Distinguish DataPro3 server software, DataPro2.5e workstation tools, FMS and the physical counters. No named-hypervisor approval established. |
| Octoplant | AMDT’s Bosch case directly describes Octoplant server/agent architecture; current product page describes its central repository. [N16] [N17] | Retain as server software with platform support not established. Customer-portal documentation is a follow-up source, not a platform compatibility code. |
| Appian 25.4+ / NextGen PAS-X | Appian requires Kubernetes-managed containers for new self-managed releases; PAS-X has a published cloud-native architecture. [A22] [S14] | Qualify application release, Kubernetes distribution and node infrastructure separately. Container support does not establish OpenShift Virtualization support. |
| Signals Notebook / SuccessFactors Learning / Vault | Published provider-hosted cores. [A19] [A81] [A21] | Customer hypervisor choice is outside the hosted core; assess local connectors/integrations separately. |

The compatibility heatmap now uses a **Supplier / Application** dropdown in place of the free-text supplier/software/roster-ID search. ## Version 1.7 — scope-aware assessment and explicit evidence age

**Compatibility subject:** the identifiable software component, appliance or protection method. A supplier does not need to sell a hypervisor to supply software that runs on one. Conversely, a supplier, equipment or service record is not itself a VM application. No company-wide claim about a supplier’s product portfolio is inferred from its inventory label.

All **47 former I entries** were reviewed against their supplied descriptions and retained research. **43** are now **W — Workload mapping required**: 7 supplier/service records, 27 equipment/OEM solution records and 9 mixed equipment/software groups. These are inventory-context records awaiting association with an assessable workload; they are not failed platform-support searches. The remaining **4** use **I — Software identity pending**, because a named software system/alias exists but its publisher/product mapping is unresolved. The complete 47-row disposition register below records the rationale for every case. The 43 W records retain their provenance and available evidence; associated software may still need qualification.

**H — Dated legacy evidence** identifies release-bound material whose present applicability is not established. It has **no universal age threshold** and does not mean end of support, incompatibility or withdrawal of vendor support. Every H-bearing matrix cell is linked to the dated evidence register below; the heatmap displays the reference date and approximate age as of **30 October 2026**. Current-looking document retrieval dates are never used as publication dates. The cover date is used where stated; partial month/year dates produce approximate ages, not invented day-level precision.

The heatmap keeps supplier/equipment context separate from platform-assessment rows and offers a dedicated inventory-context view. W rows show one mapping action across the platform area rather than twelve apparent compatibility failures. The four executive cards share the same teal accent treatment. The [archived v1.7 heatmap](../interactive/OT_Application_Support_Heatmap_2026-10-30_v1.7.html) reflects the register at that revision.

The interactive report opens with an executive summary, followed by separate navigation for the compatibility heatmap, enterprise context and source register. All 97 entries and 12 platform columns are available; filters distinguish application hosting, backup protection and provider-hosted cores. Selecting a cell exposes its exact register classification, scope, remaining confirmation and entry-level sources. The navy, teal and neutral palette follows the supplied METI V2.3 reference report.

Cohesity–VMware is **B, published backup interoperability**; HPE remains **BA, guest-agent protection**, and Proxmox remains **BR, repository/Remote Adapter workflow**. Mixed outcomes and explicit component exclusions are retained without promoting a generic policy, related product or backup method to application-hosting approval. Empty research results remain gaps rather than unsupported classifications.

The application provenance in the Enterprise Context appendix is carried into the interactive report. The source register also retains unresolved access and document-identity qualifications. The v1.5 audit sections below document the earlier research and link-check passes; they do not represent new research performed for v1.6.

## Earlier source-link validation — 30 October 2026

The pre-applicability snapshot contained147 source references /145 distinct URLs after removal of an inaccessible GE iFIX policy source and its dependent text. The earlier pinned-v1.5 audit checked148 references /146 distinct URLs:137 original URLs returned readable content and9 could not be inspected. Those figures describe that earlier snapshot, not the current source inventory. One same-document route was repaired (A23), and two unavailable routes now cite clearly labelled alternative official product-context pages (A39,A44). A51 points to the actual candidate DataLogging product page; A03/P10 use verified canonical destinations.

**Earlier source-link audit record:** five retained source URLs were unresolved: A26 (403), A50 (timeout), A60 (reader-size limit), A82 (404 with an unstable alternative), S25 (403). Their availability must not be represented as verified. The HPE N02/N04 landing-page Download links were followed to matching PDFs. S16 is a move notice/customer portal, N10 is a third-party mirror, S06 has a V21/V19 applicability conflict, and S28's visible course body does not confirm version5.1. These are not clean release-specific support certificates.

The individual outcomes, original URLs, document identities and replacements from the earlier148-reference snapshot are retained in the historical [OT_Source_Link_Audit_2026-10-30.md](OT_Source_Link_Audit_2026-10-30.md). The current document's retained-source counts and removal supersede that snapshot's inventory. The fresh applicability inspection and technical inspection record below take precedence for the three re-investigated cases; the remaining notes retain their original scope. Earlier entry research dispositions remain a record of that content-audit pass, not a claim that every source opens now. Web-reader retrieval may use indexed/cached content and is not a guarantee of browser access, entitlement or application compatibility. No support status was upgraded by this link check.

## What this roster shows

**Enterprise context and application provenance:** see the [Enterprise Context appendix](#appendix--enterprise-context) for supplied source files, declared CMDB/supplier-registry origins, coverage limits and complete row-level inventory lineage.

Every one of the **97 detailed supplier/application entries** in “OT and VMware.md” is retained, in its original category and order. All **65 entries** in “OT Applications.md” are cross-referenced below. These are source entries, not 97 distinct applications or independently verified internal deployments. Original vendor/product text is preserved; possible naming errors are not silently corrected.

**How to identify gaps:** Read the status column. A published supported configuration, conditional support and a deployment example are different outcomes. “Public support not established” means the documented live searches and source inspections did not establish sufficient public evidence. **Unresolved software identity, inaccessible documentation and an unvalidated pairing are not evidence that an alternative is unsupported.** No entry has been classified as universally unsupported.

The earlier report covered a priority subset. Version 1.5 records fresh research for every roster entry while preserving unresolved identities and public-evidence gaps.

## Reading the updated roster

All 97 original entries remain. VMware has been added without assuming support from installed use. The v1.5 audit findings are reconciled below. Limited-scope and conditional findings, unresolved software identities and support evidence gaps remain visible; there is no portfolio-wide compatibility percentage.

## Version 1.5 — full-roster public-evidence audit

**Decision use:** assess published support and prioritize vendor confirmation. **This is not an installed-estate compatibility certificate.** The Cohesity omission exposed incomplete research coverage in earlier versions. Version 1.5 replaces the earlier assurance narrative with an entry-by-entry audit record and explicit evidence limits.

**Scope completed:** all 97 roster entries received fresh live research and a recorded disposition; all 133 existing source-reference routes were reopened (including repeated URLs; access failures are recorded); application, equipment-OEM, platform and hypervisor material was considered. The original 65-group cross-reference remains intact. Searches covered the named software or supplier, virtualization/support requirements and relevant target names. Broad platform searches and primary support policies covered VMware plus the ten alternative families, with Hyper-V and Azure Local treated separately. This is complete coverage of the supplied roster, **not proof that every possible publication, customer-only matrix or platform combination has been found**.

**Authority and edit order:** this Markdown is the only authoritative research base. Future evidence or classification changes must be made and committed here first. The [current interactive heatmap](../interactive/OT_Application_Support_Heatmap_2026-10-30_v2.0.html) is generated from this register. Version1.6 remains the pre-correction snapshot; its I labels are superseded by the scope review above. This register controls any difference in interpretation.

**Evidence rules:** an application manufacturer's release/component statement establishes application support within its conditions. A hypervisor's guest-OS support or application-agnostic claim establishes a different layer. Equipment-OEM configuration, interfaces, database/OS support, hardware qualification and entitlement still have to align. Backup interoperability is recorded separately from application hosting. A SaaS core, container platform, RPA agent and VM application server are not interchangeable workloads. Two pages repeating one vendor claim are not independent validation.

| Material audit finding | v1.5 treatment |
|---|---|
| Cohesity alternatives were under-researched | Add published Hyper-V, AHV, OpenShift Virtualization and OLVM backup coverage, plus HPE VM Essentials guest-agent protection. [N01] [N02] [N03] [N04] AHV/OpenShift have platform-vendor corroboration. Proxmox is a dated repository/Remote Adapter workflow, not a native API claim. [A01] [A02] [A03] [A04] [A05] |
| Commvault evidence was anchored to an older overview | Add current HPE VM Essentials, OpenShift VM and OLVM findings, retaining release/method restrictions. [A06] [A07] [A08] [A71] [A72] [A73] |
| OnGuard core support was missed | OEM guide explicitly certifies core Server and Communication Server on ESXi; keep NVR Hyper-V evidence separate. [A74] [S31] |
| Cognex and SoftMax require component/contract boundaries | VisionPro Deep Learning has an explicit VM exclusion; SoftMax VM license language is not a hypervisor support approval, and SMPCare excludes virtualization services. [A09] [A10] [A11] |
| Generic VM policies were missed | visionCATS server-only VM permission and OnBase conditional policy are now visible; neither approves all components or the unknown internal product mapping. [A32] [A75] |
| SAP and SailPoint had unresearched component evidence | Add bounded SAP platform/HANA leads and explicit Identity Services VA platforms; do not apply these to entire grouped enterprise rows. [A12] [A13] [A15] [A76] |
| WinCC variants were insufficiently distinguished | Add current WinCC OA documentation as a related lead. It cannot qualify WinCC Unified, classic WinCC, Flexible, SIPAT or the unspecified Siemens equipment. [A80] |
| Hosting models were conflated with VM support gaps | Signals Notebook, SuccessFactors Learning and Vault have published managed-cloud models. Appian core, TrackWise versus Digital and ServiceNow deployment options require separate treatment. [A19] [A21] [A22] [A18] [A81] [A85] [A86] |

**Retained qualifications:** Nutanix brand wording in FactoryTalk, Desigo and Empower still does not prove AHV. The WinCC Unified V21 route still contains V19 installation text. PCS7/NVR and the newly recovered legacy PI policy remain versioned/historical evidence. Desigo specification wording is not a qualification matrix. The OpenLab CDS 3.0 component table was checked again against its rendered page: VMware supports AIC/client/server components; Hyper-V supports client, excludes AIC and has server TBD; workstation is excluded on both. None of this approves every Agilent instrument. [S04] [S06] [S07] [S08] [S09] [S12] [S31] [A83]

**Access limits:** Use the latest source-link validation above for current availability. A26,A50,A60,A82,S25 could not be fully inspected in this pass. A23 has a verified route repair; A39/A44 now use labelled alternate publisher product-context pages. Search snippets, portal notices, community answers and generic OS compatibility do not close application support gaps.

## Final audit revision — what changed and what was actually checked

**Completed 30 October 2026.** This revision follows the request for a fresh live-search audit. It uses the committed Markdown as the base and preserves all 97 original supplier/application identities, descriptions and 65 original group cross-references. Research coverage is distinct from proof of compatibility.

The documented search pass ran **194 fresh queries: two per roster entry**, covering manufacturer requirements and alternative-platform terms, followed by targeted searches and direct document checks. **133 existing source-reference routes (131 distinct URLs)** were reopened; three returned retrieval failures (Climet timeout, Roche reader-size limit and Cohesity 403). Readable pages that are only marketing, navigation, licensing, identity or architecture context are not support certificates. Six previously missing source shortcut definitions are repaired. New evidence and rejected leads are listed below. All dates of inspection are 30 October 2026; crawler dates are not publication dates.

| Material correction or newly tested claim | Final outcome |
|---|---|
| Cohesity on VMware | Published backup support is confirmed. The prior evidence-gap classification was wrong. [S37] [S38] |
| Cohesity on HPE VM Essentials | Newly added, with independent HPE corroboration. Protection is **guest-agent based**; exact DataProtect/build/guest match remains necessary. [N01] [N02] [N03] [N04] |
| Acronis on platforms not individually listed | Record its **generic guest-agent fallback policy**, separately from named agentless integrations. A blank native-integration result must not hide that policy. [S24] |
| Cognex | Current Deep Learning 4.2 prohibits VMs for two named components. Dell's blueprint includes ViDi inside a VM. Record the conflict; neither a universal Cognex exclusion nor universal support is justified. [N05] [N06] |
| LabX | Newer version-13 generic VM guidance found on a mirror. Primary provenance remains unverified; the 2014 disclaimer is not a present-day universal exclusion. [N10] [S17] |
| SoftMax Pro | Historical primary VM sizing guidance recovered. Indexed support-table wording does not match the current live PDF; it is not accepted as current proof. [N11] [N14] |
| Belimed / Siemens Healthcare | Related SmartHub Connect and RAPIDComm publications permit generic virtual servers. Installed-product mapping and named hypervisors remain unresolved. [N07] [N08] |
| Thomas | Previously blocked control-upgrade page now readable. It supplies product context, not hypervisor approval. [A67] |

**Leadership interpretation:** the matrix below is a record of published evidence, including unresolved cases. It is not a migration approval, a certification of the installed estate, or a ranking by raw green-cell count. No assumption of support is made from shared KVM technology, supported guest OS, VMware installation history, cloud hosting, database support, backups or successful VM import. A product-specific restriction takes precedence over a general policy for that same release/component.

## Complete roster–platform evidence matrix

This is the technical record of published VM support or backup interoperability if that deployment/protection method is selected. These codes do not state whether a hypervisor is required. Use the execution statements at the start of the register for that question.

Every one of the 97 roster entries has an explicit disposition for each of 12 platform columns (**1,164 cells**). Read the evidence/scope column and full roster before using a cell. A status concerns only the component the source actually names, never all products of a supplier.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| S | Manufacturer names the platform for a defined component/configuration; installed-release matching is still required. |
| C | Named conditional or best-effort support policy, with restrictions. |
| B | Published backup interoperability; not application-hosting approval. The detailed register controls method and versions. |
| BA | Explicit guest-agent protection; not hypervisor-level image backup. |
| BR | Dated repository/Remote Adapter backup workflow, not native image API integration. |
| G | A generic VM or guest-agent policy is available; this particular named platform/build pairing remains unvalidated. This is not a platform-specific support claim. |
| L | Related product, dependency, engineering tool or platform deployment only; original workload approval remains open. |
| H | Dated legacy evidence for the cited release/component; see the historical evidence register for publication date, approximate age and exact scope. Present applicability is not established. No age cutoff or end-of-support conclusion is implied. |
| X | Explicit exclusion, only for the component/release specified in the evidence. Mixed cells retain both outcomes. |
| U | Live research did not establish qualifying named-platform support. It does not mean unsupported. |
| I | A named software system/alias is present, but its actual publisher/product/edition mapping is pending. This is a software-identity issue, not a classification for equipment or supplier/service records. |
| W | Workload mapping required: the inventory record names equipment, a supplier/service or a mixed group rather than a separately assessable software workload. Platform compatibility is not classified at this record level; identify the underlying software and node role first. |
| N | Provider-hosted core; customer hypervisor choice does not apply to that core. Adjuncts remain separate. |

Azure Local is separate from Hyper-V. AHV means AHV, not any Nutanix-hosted ESXi deployment. HPE means the HVM hypervisor, not HPE management of VMware. Oracle means OLVM/Oracle Linux KVM, not Oracle VM. Canonical means MicroCloud/OpenStack with the exact distribution unvalidated unless stated; generic OpenStack references do not close it. Citrix XenServer is not XCP-ng.

## Historical evidence dates and release boundaries

The dates and document versions below come from the retained source records/captures. Ages are calculated at month precision against 30 October 2026. H is an applicability qualification, not an age-based support lifecycle policy. These entries cover every H-bearing matrix cell. The current-scope OnGuard core document is shown separately to distinguish it from the H-labelled NVR component.

| History ID | Source | Document date | Approximate age at 30 October 2026 | Product / release | Matrix use and boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HT01 | A83 | November 2010 | 15 years 11 months (about 16 years) | OSIsoft Virtualization and the PI System, version 1.2 | OT001 VMware / Hyper-V H only. Cover says November 2010; the internal imprint also says Published: 4.10.2009. The cover revision date is used, not a guessed interpretation of the older imprint. This is not a current PI/RtReports release certificate. |
| HT02 | N14 | October 2011 | About 15 years | SoftMax Pro version 6 user guide, document 5014177 C | OT062 all H/U cells. Generic VM sizing for this old release; no named-platform approval or current-version support inferred. |
| HT03 | S31 | January 2022 | About 4 years 9 months | LenelS2 NVR 7.8, DOC-909 revision 12.002 | OT082 VMware S/H and Hyper-V L/H: H refers to NVR 7.8, not the entire OnGuard core. Current NVR lifecycle/entitlement remains unestablished. |
| HT04 | S19 | August 2022 | About 4 years 2 months | WinCC V7.5 SP2 / Professional V17; application example v2.1 | OT010 VMware L/H, related software only. This does not approve all Siemens products or Hyper-V. |
| HT05 | S07 | October 2022 | About 4 years | PCS 7 V9.1 SP2 virtualization manual | Additional legacy component context for OT010 VMware L/H; do not transfer it to an unspecified WinCC/SIPAT/QBMS installation. |
| HT06 | A74 | September 2024 | About 2 years 1 month | OnGuard Advanced Installation Topics, DOC-100 revision 14.027 | Dated S-side context for OT082 VMware S/H: core Server / Communication Server ESXi scope. H in this mixed cell refers to the separate January 2022 NVR source, not an automatic downgrade of this core statement. |

The November 2025 PI Software Schedule (N09) discusses licensing and does not renew the November 2010 technical policy. Historical restrictions elsewhere, such as LabX 2014 (S17), remain explicitly tied to their releases. For the Waters FR5 historical limitation (S10), the retained record does not establish a publication date; its age cannot be calculated and must not be guessed from the release name. No evidence is considered current merely because its URL was opened in 2026.

## Inventory scope review — every former I entry

This is a classification of each **supplied record’s scope**, using its original description and the retained evidence. It is not a newly researched statement that the company never sells software or can never support a virtualized deployment. W means that the inventory must first map the record to its software workload(s). I is retained only for an actual software label/alias whose product mapping is pending. All original source identities and descriptions remain unchanged in the master list and Enterprise Context appendix.

**Reconciliation:** 47 former I entries = 43 W inventory-context records + 4 I software-identity records. W roles = 4 systems-integration/engineering + 3 supply/software-service/hosting + 27 equipment/OEM solutions + 9 mixed equipment/software groups. All other 50 roster entries retain their prior classifications and evidence. The 43×12 recodings are changes in assessment scope, not newly discovered platform support.

Each entry below records what the primary text can establish and what the triangulation cannot establish. Publication/revision and inspection dates are different. A second publisher is recorded only where actually inspected; an application-agnostic platform manual does not supply missing application approval. The existing source registers retain detailed component/release conditions.

Exact patch/API/storage/restore support still requires the current matrix. |

### Triangulation outcome

Application-owner evidence, equipment-OEM evidence, platform integration and guest OS/hardware support were checked as separate layers. Positive cross-publisher corroboration is present for Cohesity–AHV/OpenShift/HPE and Commvault–HPE. Many other findings remain single-publisher technical policies or related-product leads; they are explicitly labelled rather than described as independently triangulated. For the remaining roster, the entry-specific gaps and query record below document what was researched. Lack of a public hit is never converted to X.

## Master list

The VMware column records the application-manufacturer position or limited related evidence, not a VMware/Broadcom certification of the complete equipment. The alternative column identifies only what the evidence permits. All ten candidate families remain in scope: Hyper-V/Azure Local, Nutanix AHV/NCI, OpenShift Virtualization, HPE HVM, Proxmox, Oracle Linux KVM/OLVM, Canonical MicroCloud/OpenStack, Scale HyperCore, Vates/XCP-ng and VergeOS. A finding on one does not establish the others. Exact version gaps are stated explicitly.

## Product-level findings that must stay separate

- **PlantPAx 5.50 / Nutanix AHV:** explicit manufacturer support, but PlantPAx is not automatically the installed platform for every Rockwell or FTView entry. S03.
- **Agilent OpenLab CDS 3.0 / Hyper-V:** client supported; workstation and AIC not supported; server components TBD. This separate product result is not support for the listed Vaya/Cary/dissolution/gateway components. S12.
- **Siemens Desigo CC V9:** Nutanix platform-family wording is not an exact AHV-build certificate. S04.
- **Siemens TIA Portal:** assigned to Siemens in the research; the original Rockwell entry is preserved so the naming discrepancy remains traceable. S05.
- **OEM WinCC/iFIX equipment:** software-level evidence must be matched to the exact OEM-supplied release, drivers, topology and equipment support terms.
- **Backup coverage:** protecting a VM does not establish application-manufacturer support for that VM's hypervisor.

## Evidence and review controls

Version 1.5 uses fresh live research across the full roster and reopens the existing source routes. S01–S38 retain their meanings; A-series references add application/OEM evidence and P-series references document platform support boundaries. All 97 entries and 65 original cross-references are preserved. The disposition log separates readable primary material, access limits and unresolved searches. Original uploaded inventories remain unchanged.

## V1.5 audit coverage and unresolved work

The following log is part of the canonical Markdown. **P** means readable primary material was inspected (possibly only a related product or identity page); **A** means a relevant route was access-limited; **Q** means fresh searches did not yield a securely matched, inspectable primary support statement. These are research dispositions, not support ratings. A P record may still be unresolved. A/Q does not mean unsupported. All checks below were performed on 30 October 2026.

Coverage: **97/97 entries**; P **78**, A **3**, Q **16**. Multi-component rows may still contain unresolved software even where one component has a P result. No compatibility percentage is calculated.

| Entry | Disposition | Fresh research scope | Result and primary evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| OT001 | P | PI virtualization policy and current AVEVA routes | Historical ESX/Hyper-V guidance remains bounded to the legacy PI guide. The current PI Software Schedule permits virtual client-tool licensing but is not technical hypervisor approval. No exact current RtReports release matrix was validated. [A83] [N09] |
| OT002 | A | octoplant server virtualization and AMDT requirements | The official documentation route remains an access notice. No current server/agent virtualization matrix was inspectable. [S16] |
| OT003 | Q | CIM PM-Server alarm SQL audit trail | The supplied vendor/acronym could not be tied securely to a manufacturer product/release. Siemens PM-SERVER search hits are not silently substituted.  |

## Platform and hypervisor triangulation

Triangulation tests whether the application statement, platform statement and component scope agree. It does not manufacture an application certificate from a guest-OS list. Stronger two-party examples are Cohesity/AHV (A01+A02), Cohesity/OpenShift (A01+A03/A04), Commvault/HPE (A07+A08), and Cohesity/HPE (N01+N02+N03+N04). Same-vendor requirement and installation pages are complementary, not independent endorsements. Most OT equipment rows lack enough release/topology identity to complete a two-sided match.

| Platform column | Primary routes checked | What the evidence does and does not establish |
|---|---|---|
| VMware | [P01] | Guest OS/hardware and SVVP conditions are separate from application-vendor support; ESXi presence is not OT approval. |
| Hyper-V | [P02] | Microsoft support policy covers named Microsoft software and validated virtualization; it does not certify third-party OT software. |
| Azure Local | [P03] | Separate deployment/VM management product; do not inherit application support from a Hyper-V statement. |
| Nutanix AHV | [P04] | Supported guest OS may allow applications without extra AHV qualification under Nutanix policy; this does not create an ISV/OEM support promise. |
| OpenShift Virtualization | [P05] [P06] | Workload compatibility and guest-OS support tiers are distinguished. Container certification alone is not a VM application approval. |
| HPE VM Essentials/HVM | [P07] [A07] [A08] [N01] [N03] [N04] | HVM versus managed VMware is distinct. Commvault image-based and Cohesity agent-based integration are corroborated; no portfolio-wide OT application approval follows. |
| Proxmox VE | [P08] | KVM/LXC platform capability is not application certification. Acronis/Commvault backup and WinCC OA evidence have separate product boundaries. |
| Oracle Linux KVM/OLVM | [P09] [S27] | Guest OS lists and ISV catalog existence are not a named application certificate. OLVM, legacy Oracle VM and generic KVM are distinct. |
| Canonical MicroCloud/OpenStack | [P10] [P11] | MicroCloud and OpenStack are different stacks. Generic OpenStack backup API support does not establish a Canonical distribution/version pairing. |
| Scale HyperCore | [P12] | Platform support scope/guest configuration is documented; application-OEM support remains separate. Acronis evidence is backup only. |
| Vates/XCP-ng | [P13] | Guest templates/tools and hypervisor support do not qualify an application. Citrix XenServer support must not be transferred to XCP-ng. |
| VergeOS | [P14] | Documentation/architecture checked. No qualifying exact master-application support statement established; VM import is not application support. |

## Outstanding confirmation for an installed-system decision

For each unresolved row obtain the actual software publisher/product/build, node role (controller/acquisition/server/client), guest OS/database, instrument interfaces and topology, target hypervisor/build, host hardware and applicable support agreement. Request a written response that names the supported production configuration, exceptions and support owner. Obtain customer-only matrices through existing entitlements where public evidence is insufficient. Validate the actual migration and restore workflow before production use.

Version 1.5 closes the *roster research-coverage step*. It does not close every support or deployment-identity gap. No claim is made that public searches can prove that nothing has been missed. Sources can change; release-specific evidence and manufacturer confirmation remain the basis for deployment decisions.

## Reproducible live-search record — final revision

These are the actual 194 initial fresh queries, run 30 October 2026, followed by direct source opens and targeted follow-ups. Quoted supplier/product terms come from the canonical roster; internal names may not resolve publicly. The alternative-platform query combines candidate terms and therefore cannot prove an exhaustive search of every release or customer-only portal. Final U/I/A results are bounded evidence dispositions, not claims that no documentation exists anywhere.

## Appendix — Enterprise Context

### Why these applications are in scope

The application scope comes from two supplied internal compilations: **EC01, OT Applications.md**, and **EC02, OT and VMware.md**. EC01 states that its roster was consolidated from Global Supplier Management & Audit Trackers and CMDB system inventories; EC02 expands the roster and adds internal identifiers and architecture context. The research retained the complete EC02 roster and reconciled every EC01 group. External vendor and hypervisor publications were then used to assess published support for those supplied entries.

**Provenance boundary:** these are supplied internal documents, not direct extracts independently obtained from the underlying CMDB, supplier registry, audit repositories or SharePoint. The source documents do not supply extract dates, record owners, underlying record URLs or record-level lineage to each named registry. Their statements establish the supplied basis for this research; they do not independently verify current installation, ownership, criticality, licensing or production architecture. The roster counts below refer to source entries, which can combine suppliers, products, equipment and services.

### Input register and deduplication

| Context ID | Supplied file | Unique contribution and treatment |
|---|---|---|
| EC01 | OT Applications.md | 65 grouped entries across six operational categories; describes supplier/audit and site-inventory origins, site examples and an explicit Animal Health coverage exclusion. All 65 groups remain in the original-entry cross-reference. |
| EC02 | OT and VMware.md | 97 detailed entries across the same six categories; supplies CSID/CS identifiers where present, additional supplier/application entries, internal solution aliases and VMware dependency context. This is the detailed roster baseline, not a public support certificate. |
| EC03 | OT_Master_Roster_Alternative_Support_2026-10-30.md — attached copy | Earlier v1.5 research snapshot. Its roster and research content are already represented in the current master; it adds no new inventory entries. Later source-link corrections take precedence. |
| EC04 | OT_Master_Roster_Alternative_Support_2026-10-30(1).md — attached copy | Byte-for-byte identical to EC03 (223,201 bytes each). Counted as one research snapshot, with no duplicate application rows or duplicated evidence. |

The supplied copies of EC01/EC02 in the project sources match the attached inventory files byte for byte. The current master remains the editing base. Superseded support assertions and inaccessible-source material from earlier report copies are not reinstated by this appendix.

### Enterprise records and coverage stated by the inputs

| Stated source or coverage | Where the input says this | What it contributes to scope |
|---|---|---|
| CMDB OT System Services, table `cmdb_ci_ot_system_service` | EC02, opening Sources declaration | Declared system-service inventory origin; record-level extracts were not supplied. |
| Global DMMD Supplier Transparency (ST) Registry | EC02, opening Sources declaration | Declared supplier-registry origin; not an independently inspected deployment list. |
| Global Supplier Management & Audit Trackers; IT/OT Audit Trackers | EC01, opening paragraph; EC02, Sources declaration | Declared supplier/audit coverage. EC01 cites West Point, Wilson and Durham as tracker site examples. These examples are not a complete site-by-application mapping. |
| Dublin Biotech (SDB) CMDB/system inventory | EC01, opening paragraph; EC02, Sources declaration | Declared site-inventory contribution. |
| Singapore (SI7) CMDB/system inventory | EC01, opening paragraph; EC02, Sources declaration | Declared site-inventory contribution. |
| Internal Hypervisor Strategy Documentation | EC02, opening Sources declaration | Declared source of enterprise virtualization context; no separate strategy document or approval record accompanied that declaration. |
| Human Health Biotech, Small Molecule and Global DMMD | EC01, final coverage note | Stated coverage of the compiled roster; no claim of complete enterprise or Animal Health coverage follows. |
| Upper Hutt and Intervet Singapore Animal Health inventory files | EC01, final coverage note | EC01 reports that these SharePoint files returned “file not found” during its compilation. Animal Health coverage is therefore incomplete; this is a limitation reported by EC01, not a fresh SharePoint access check. |

### How the application roster was assembled

The 65 EC01 groups map to **83 of the 97 detailed EC02 entries**. Some groups split into several supplier rows, and EC02 also contains **14 additional entries** without an EC01 group. Thus 65 and 97 are different levels of inventory granularity, not two independent application populations. The full 65-group mapping remains in [Cross-reference to every original OT Applications.md entry](#cross-reference-to-every-original-ot-applicationsmd-entry); the row-level lineage below locates all 97 entries in EC02.

| Operational category | EC01 groups | EC02/master entries | Master IDs |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Global core OT, automation, DCS and MES | 11 | 11 | OT001–OT011 |
| Bioprocessing and production skids | 14 | 17 | OT012–OT028 |
| Packaging, serialization and vision | 4 | 4 | OT029–OT032 |
| Clean utilities, water and facility controls | 6 | 10 | OT033–OT042 |
| Laboratory OT, analytical instruments and QC | 22 | 30 | OT043–OT072 |
| Enterprise OT support, EAM, validation and resilience | 8 | 25 | OT073–OT097 |
| **Total source entries** | **65** | **97** | **All retained** |

The 14 EC02-only entries are **OT054 IPC, OT055 LabWare, OT057 Mason Technology / Midwest Information Systems, OT075 AJJITEC, OT076 AliHealth, OT078 AWS, OT080 Cohesity, OT084 Hyland, OT086 Global Solutions, OT088 OpenText, OT089 Oracle & SailPoint, OT092 SAP, OT093 ServiceNow and OT095 SuccessFactors**. They remain in scope because they appear in the supplied detailed inventory; absence from EC01 does not establish absence from the enterprise.

### Unique enterprise context carried forward from EC02

These are **statements reported by the supplied internal source**. They explain why these workloads matter to the Cloud leader; they do not change any audited support status or establish an independently verified deployment plan.

| Enterprise context from EC02 | Source locator | Implication for this assessment |
|---|---|---|
| The estate spans DC Critical, Edge Enterprise/Jump, and Shop Floor / Lab Floor Networks (SFN/LFN); EC02 describes vSphere, ESXi, vCenter and vSAN as foundational infrastructure. | §1, opening paragraph | Assess the workload/node and deployment tier, rather than treating every supplier as one movable VM application. No tier-by-record mapping was supplied. |
| DC Critical is described as having high VMware dependency, particularly an Emerson DeltaV stretch cluster and a “Data Center in-a-Rack” configuration. | §1, DC Critical bullet; §2, Emerson row | Match actual topology, release, hardware and support terms. The public DeltaV evidence in OT004 does not independently confirm this internal production VMware architecture. |
| Edge SFN/LFN changes are described as paced by OT vendor support matrices and version qualification. | §1, Edge bullet | Match exact application/OEM configurations and target versions; a technically viable platform alone does not close the application support gap. |
| AWS EVS is described as selected for MES dev/test, CTC DR and AME migration. | §1, Cloud-Hosted VMware bullet; §2, PAS-X row | Keep these internal workload/use-case statements distinct from externally established EVS platform facts in OT078. Selection, implementation status and the meanings of CTC/AME were not independently established. |
| EC02 associates SCADA, PAT/SIPAT and qualified building management with site clusters, and PI/RtReports with time-series aggregation on Windows servers. | §2, Siemens and AVEVA rows | Separate products, server roles and releases. These statements describe source-reported dependencies, not blanket certification. |
| Laboratory client/server acquisition and instrument control are described as site-VM workloads, while some local instrument controllers remain physical OEM PCs. | §2, Waters/Mettler Toledo/Agilent row | Distinguish application/database servers, acquisition nodes and physical device interfaces; backup protection does not establish that instrument control can migrate. |
| Octoplant is described as supporting PLC/SCADA version-control and recovery operations. | §2, AUVESY-MDT row | Treat its operational role separately from the current access gap in its server/agent support documentation (OT002). |
| EC02 states an intended transition from Commvault to Cohesity by the end of 2026. | §2, backup row | Retain as a source-reported plan only. No programme approval, completion status or scope was supplied; OT080/OT081 assess published protection methods separately. |
| EC02 frames changes within GxP qualification and refers to EU GMP Annex 11/15 and GAMP 5. | §1, governance paragraph and DC Critical bullet | Obtain the applicable site quality/change-control decision and validation scope. This appendix does not turn the source's wording into a legal determination or a universal requirement for full requalification. |

The earlier EC02 compatibility matrix contains broad supplier/platform assertions that were tested and qualified by the current research. Its VMware-dependency narrative is retained here as enterprise context; support conclusions remain those in the current master and evidence matrix. The original grouping of TIA Portal under Rockwell, ambiguous supplier/software identities and OEM/software boundaries remain visible in the research rather than being silently normalized.

### Provenance handoff for an installed-estate decision

The supplied documents establish **why each research entry was included** and preserve its source label and supplied identifier. To validate the actual estate, the next handoff needs the underlying record/export, extraction date, owning team/site, actual software/component and release, current hosting location and criticality, plus the applicable vendor/OEM support and quality approvals. These fields were not inferred from registry names or public vendor pages.

**Appendix reconciliation:** all 97 EC02 supplier labels and descriptions match the existing master in source order; all 65 EC01 groups remain mapped; the two attached report copies are deduplicated; no inventory entry, support classification or external evidence reference is changed by this appendix.

Page 2: client/DAS/SQL architecture. Legacy Windows table, no named-hypervisor approval. Publisher PDF route timed out. |
| N19 | [Roche Cedex HiRes current product page][N19] | Directly opened manufacturer page. Confirms an instrument supplied with software/accessories. Current announcement says ordering ends in 2026 and reagents/consumables/spares/application support continue until March 2029. Neither announcement establishes VM hosting approval. |
| N20 | [Roche-authored Cedex HiRes 2020 addendum — republished copy][N20] | Roche-authored Addendum 6, October 2020, software 2.3.2, guide 3.0; republished by Manuzoid. Cover/copyright and Revised Chapter XI, p. 4 inspected; host chatbot ignored. Roche control-unit/image guidance and limited other-hardware support. Original A60 package remains incompletely retrieved; no universal VM ban inferred. |
| N21 | [Industrial Automation Systems company profile][N21] | Directly opened same-name company’s primary description of custom control-system design/build and commissioning. Supports supplier-role context; does not independently match the Wilson contract or identify its delivered software. |
| N22 | [RoviSys control systems integration][N22] | Directly opened manufacturer/integrator capabilities: PLC/DCS/DDC/SCADA integration and delivered HMI/control services. An integration engagement is not a single hostable product. |
| N23 | [Alfa Laval Culturefuge system architecture][N23] | Directly opened equipment-manufacturer page; describes a skid-mounted system with integrated PLC/electrical and pneumatic equipment. Related family context, not proof of the installed model or external software/VM support. |
| N24 | [Freund-Vector COMPU4 process information][N24] | Directly opened two-page manufacturer brochure, Open System Architecture section: Allen-Bradley/Siemens PLC and iFIX/InTouch/RSView SCADA choices. Legacy COMPU4 document; publication date not established from its contents. No VM approval or installed COMPU release match inferred. |
| N25 | [PerkinElmer Frontier/Spectrum technical FAQ][N25] | Directly opened manufacturer Japanese technical FAQ. Identifies Frontier/Spectrum instrument software and version-specific Windows OS support, including Spectrum IR 10.7.2. OS support is not a VM certificate and does not identify the separately listed ELN. |
| N26 | [Advanced Instruments OsmoTECH XT user guide — republished page][N26] | Manufacturer-authored User Guide Appendix F, printed p. 89, republished by EasyManual. Opened instrument data-management page: touchscreen, onboard records, embedded web server, network/OPC-UA integration. A network connection/embedded web server does not make the instrument a customer-hosted VM server. Applies to this OsmoTECH XT lead, not the entire grouped supplier row. |

## Technical inspection record — evidence availability is not compatibility

This record is retained for auditability. It does not appear as a platform status or executive heatmap legend. The fresh inspection obtained useful deployment evidence without claiming that the original Octoplant knowledge base, Climet publisher routes or full Roche package were made universally accessible.

| Original case | Fresh inspection outcome | Still open |
|---|---|---|
| Octoplant S16 | Rechecked the move notice/myAMDT route and opened N16/N17 manufacturer architecture. | Release-specific technical requirements require the applicable knowledge-base/owner evidence. No named-platform approval inferred. |
| Climet A50 | Rechecked publisher software/brochure routes and inspected the complete manufacturer brochure excerpts on N18, including architecture. | Exact installed software/version and current OEM virtualization policy. Publisher routes still timed out; a host-generated product summary was not used. |
| Roche A60 | Rechecked original package; inspected relevant Roche-authored 2020 addendum sections and imprint on N20, and current primary product information N19. | Exact installed software/version and OEM approval for alternate hardware/hosting. Original full package retrieval remains limited; no claim of complete original-PDF review. |

The retained baseline still contains historical access notes and audit dispositions. They describe earlier attempts and are superseded for current applicability by this inspection record. Readable route retrieval can use cached/indexed content and is not an independent HTTP/browser-access guarantee. All97 rows received a deployment-scope disposition; public evidence did not identify every installed software component. No claim of complete installed-estate compatibility or exhaustive publication discovery is made.
